    An additional 780,000 barrels exited from the Strategic
Petroleum Reserves, extending declines for a 28th-straight week
and putting stocks there down to 282.98 million barrels, a fresh
44-year low.

    Commercial crude stocks declined across all regions outside
of the Midwest, where planned and unplanned refinery outages
have trimmed local demand. Midwest stocks climbed 1.87 million
barrels, EIA data showed, including a 440,000-barrel build in
inventories at the NYMEX delivery hub of Cushing, Oklahoma, that
left stockpiles there at a 20-week high.

    Midwest refinery utilization climbed 2.6 percentage points
to 88.2% of capacity but remained well below the September
average of around 94% of capacity. On Sept. 26, ExxonMobil
restarted its 267,000 barrels per day Joliet refinery in Illinois
after a power outage shut the plant unexpectedly on Sept. 13,
according to a company spokesperson.

    Chevron Corp. and Shell have started separately shutting in

production at their Gulf of Mexico facilities in preparation for
Tropical Storm Isaias, they said Oct. 7, adding that all associated
personnel are also being evacuated.

    While some additional personnel are being transported
onshore, production from four facilities have been shut in, while
its other five Gulf of Mexico facilities remain at normal levels,
Chevron said without identifying the offshore production units.

    "At our onshore facilities, we continue to follow our
established storm response procedures and are closely
monitoring the projected path of the storm," it said.

    Continuing the upward trajectory seen in recent months,

threshold in December 2022.

    State carrier Sovcomflot's tankers loaded 6.4 million
barrels in September, while the remainder were generally
lifted by tankers owned by small, little-known companies,
according to the CAS and MIRS data. All of the ships were
flagged in Russia.

Bulgarian waters
    In a separate incident, two civilian vessels were attacked

in Bulgarian waters, with one sinking and the other sustaining
significant damage, Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said
on X Oct. 6. Zelenskyy said Ukraine's Defence Forces conducted
no operations in the area and attributed the attack to Russia,
saying it was "a combined Russian attack using maritime and
aerial drones."

    One of the vessels struck was identified as MV Royad
Mammadov in an Oct. 6 statement from India's Ministry of
External Affairs. The ministry confirmed that two foreign
seafarers were killed in the attack and that crew members,
including Indian nationals, were injured.
